Subject- Submission of Annual Report of M/s South West Pinnacle Exploration Limited for the Financial Year 2025-26 
Dear Sir/Madam,  
The 20th Annual General Meeting (“AGM”) of the Company will be held on Monday, 28th September, 2026 at 02:30 P.M (IST) 
through Video Conferencing/Other Audio Visual Means  in accordance with the applicable circulars issued by the Ministry of 
Corporate Affairs (“MCA”) and the Securities and Exchange Board of India (“SEBI”). 
Pursuant to Regulation 30  read with Regulation 34 of the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 
2015 (“Listing Regulations”), please find enclosed the 20th Annual Report of the Company for FY 2025-26, together with the Notice 
convening the 20th AGM. 
The Notice of 20th AGM along with the Annual Report FY 2025-26 has been sent electronically to all the Members of the Company, 
whose e-mail ids are registered with the Company, the Registrar & Transfer Agent (RTA) or the Depositories and also being made 
available on the website of the Company at www.southwestpinnacle.com  
Further, in Compliance with Regulation 36(1)(b) of the SEBI Listing Regulations and other applicable provisions, the Company has 
issued letters to those shareholders whose e -mail addresses are not registered with the Company/Registrar & Transfer 
Agent/Depository Participants, providi ng the weblink of Company’s website from where the Integrated Annual Report for FY 
2025-26 can be accessed.  
The Annual Report containing Notice of 20th Annual General Meeting and other details is also uploaded on the Company’s  
website at www.southwestpinnacle.com. 
This is for your information and record.   
 
Thanking you,

Chairman & Managing  
Director's Message
MR. VIKAS JAIN 
Chairman & Managing Director
Dear shareholders, 
 I am pleased to share that FY2026 was a landmark year 
for the Company, wherein we achieved our highest-ever 
annual performance in terms of  revenue and 
profitability, reflecting strong execution and operational 
momentum. During Financial Year 2026, the operating 
revenue was INR 243 crores, which increased by 35% 
year-on-year. The EBITDA stood at INR 58 crores, an 
increase of  74% year-on-year, translating into an 
EBITDA margin of  23.99%. The net profit after tax 
grew nearly 101% year-on-year to INR 33 crore, with a 
PAT margin of  13.58%.
It is pertinent to mention here that this performance is 
despite dynamic and often challenging business 
environment. During this challenging time, we have 
expanded our footprint, strengthened client 
relationships, and enhanced our capabilities across 
multiple domains of  mineral exploration and drilling. 
Today, the importance of  mineral exploration cannot be 
undermined. In a world shaped by increasing geopolitical 
uncertainties, supply chain disruptions, and the global 
race for critical minerals, countries are re-evaluating their 
resource strategies. India, with its growing economic 
ambitions and infrastructure needs, must ensure reliable 
access to minerals that power industries, energy 
transition, and technological advancement. 
Exploration is the foundation of  this strategy. Without 
robust and scientific exploration, there can be no 
sustainable mining, no resource security, and no long-
term industrial growth. Our work directly supports this 
national priority—helping identify new reserves, 
improve resource estimation, and reduce dependence on 
imports. 
Over the past year, we have invested significantly in 
modern technologies, advanced geophysical techniques, 
and data-driven exploration methods. We have also 
focused on strengthening our talent pool, ensuring that 
our teams remain at the cutting edge of  innovation and 
execution excellence. 
In line with our growth strategy, we operated across the 
highest number of  exploration domains during the year, 
with a presence across six active domains, while also 
expanding our geographic footprint across eight states in 
India. We also maintained an impeccable safety record 
with zero injuries across all operations during the year.
It is heartening to inform that during the year, we have 
also been notified as an accredited prospecting agency by 
the Ministry of  Coal, Government of  India, for carrying 
out prospecting operations related to coal and lignite 
exploration. Accordingly, exploration activities at our 
coal block in Jharkhand were undertaken at full speed 
which has now been completed paving way for early 
development of  the same, our second business stream. 
The GR and mining plan preparation and other related 
activities are now being put on fast track mode and 
hopeful of  commencing the coal production by start of  
FY 28-29. 
Our overseas operations in Oman, the third business 
stream, is also progressing with good pace. In Oman out 
of  two JVs, the first JV is imparting mineral services thru 
Annual Report 2025-26  Ü I
[page 6]
an 11 years' contract valuing USD 125 Million, besides 
providing exploration services to various local clients. 
Our resources are currently booked for next two years. 
Besides, new opportunities are also on the anvil which 
will further strengthen our operations there.
In second JV we have been allocated a large mineral block 
spread across 1400 Sq KM, having minerals like silver, 
gold , copper , basalt and chromite etc., for exploration to 
be  followed by exploitation by Ministry of  Energy and 
Minerals, Sultanate of  Oman. The air borne survey has 
since been completed and other exploration activities are 
presently going on at full swing.
To strengthen our long-term international growth 
strategy, we have made strategic investment in an 
Australian publicly listed minerals exploration and 
development company having primary interests in Oman 
and other Middle East regions. 
All in all, our all three verticals of  our business are going 
on well and smoothly and with increased impetus of  the 
Government on exploration and energy security, we 
expect to be on an excellent growth trajectory and are 
confident of  achieving around 20 % growth year on year 
in short to medium term with substantial increase in 
bottom line.  
Our ratios improved significantly during the year, with 
Return on Equity improving from 10% to 16%, while 
Return on Capital Employed increased from 16% to 
23%, reflecting improved profitability and efficient 
capital utilization.
Looking ahead, our priorities are clear:
Ÿ To strengthen operational capacity and technology 
adoption 
Ÿ To deepen our presence in emerging exploration 
segments 
Ÿ To maintain industry-leading safety standards 
Ÿ To improve margins through better execution 
efficiency
Ÿ And above all, to deliver sustainable value to our 
shareholders.
Keeping above in mind, we remain focused on expanding 
our capabilities, maintaining execution excellence, and 
creating sustainable value for all stakeholders.
With our diversified technical capabilities, experienced 
manpower, fleet strength, and pan-India presence, we are 
strategically positioned to benefit from this expanding 
national focus and improve further our performance in 
coming years.
Lastly and most importantly, I express my sincere 
gratitude to our clients, employees, bankers, partners, 
regulators, and all shareholders for your trust and 
continued support. The success of  South West Pinnacle 
is the result of  collective commitment. We assure you that 
we will continue to build this company with discipline, 
integrity, and long-term vision.
Jai Hind, 
Vikas Jain 
Chairman & Managing Director

MR. PIYUSH JAIN
Joint Managing Director
Joint Managing  
Director's Message
Dear Shareholders, 
It is my privilege to present to you the progress and 
achievements of  your Company during the year. This year 
marks an important chapter in the growth journey of  South 
West Pinnacle Exploration Limited, and I am pleased to share 
that your Company has delivered one of  its strongest 
performances since inception, across revenue, profitability, 
operational execution and safety.
This performance assumes even greater significance 
considering the challenging operating environment faced by 
the industry, including increasing execution complexities, 
difficult and remote project locations and rising cost pressures. 
Despite these challenges, your Company continued to adapt, 
execute and grow stronger. Our performance reflects the 
resilience of  our business model, the strength of  our execution 
capabilities and most importantly, the continued confidence 
that our clients place in us.
Over the years, South West Pinnacle has established itself  as 
one of  India's trusted integrated exploration service providers, 
delivering specialised drilling and exploration services across 
coal and non-coal minerals, oil & gas, coal bed methane, aquifer 
mapping and geological & geophysical services.
We have developed strong capabilities across core drilling, 
reverse circulation drilling, large diameter drilling, 
underground drilling, geological and geophysical surveys and 
other specialised exploration services. More importantly, we 
have demonstrated the ability to deploy these capabilities 
efficiently across some of  the most challenging geographies — 
from remote and inaccessible mineral belts to complex terrains 
where technical expertise, precision and execution discipline 
are critical.
We have successfully executed over 165 projects and are 
currently executing 20 projects across multiple regions and 
exploration domains. During the year, our strong performance 
was driven by consistent project execution, improved 
operational efficiency and a well-diversified order mix. Our 
fleet of  over 45 rigs and associated resources remained 
substantially deployed, enabling us to maintain execution 
momentum and deliver projects within committed timelines.
Our operational capabilities and market reputation have 
translated into a robust order book, providing strong revenue 
visibility for the coming years. A significant milestone during 
the year was securing our largest-ever single order, valued at 
over ₹300 crores, from a renowned metals and minerals  group 
company. Along with other order wins, this has taken our total 
order book to approximately ₹761 crore, one of  the strongest 
levels in the history of  the Company.
The strength of  this order book is not limited to its size. Its 
composition has also improved significantly, with more than 
two-thirds of  the order book coming from private-sector 
clients. We expect this to contribute towards stronger cash 
flows, improved working-capital efficiency and greater 
execution flexibility.
Importantly, our current project portfolio also provides 
meaningful operational continuity during the monsoon season, 
traditionally a period when field operations can face 
disruptions. This should enable us to maintain better execution 
consistency across the year.
With India accelerating exploration of  coal, critical and 
strategic minerals and other natural resources to strengthen 
domestic resource and energy security, we believe South West 
Pinnacle is well positioned to participate meaningfully in this 
national growth opportunity.
One of  our greatest strengths continues to be the trust of  our 
clients. In our industry, repeat business is perhaps the strongest 
Annual Report 2025-26  Ü III
[page 8]
endorsement of  performance. We are proud that many of  our 
major clients continue to engage with us project after project 
and year after year.
Such relationships can only be sustained through consistent 
delivery on quality, timelines, technical competence, safety and 
operational integrity. The long-standing relationships we have 
built with leading public and private sector organisations are a 
testimony to the confidence placed in our people, our 
capabilities and our systems. We remain committed to 
strengthening these relationships while continuing to expand 
our client base and enter new areas of  opportunity.
Underground coal gasification is emerging as an important 
strategic opportunity in India and represents an area where we 
see considerable potential for the future. India is increasingly 
looking beyond the conventional use of  coal for power 
generation and towards its conversion into higher-value 
products through gasification.
Given South West Pinnacle's extensive experience in coal 
exploration, drilling, coal bed methane and associated 
subsurface services, we believe that underground coal 
gasification can emerge as an important new growth vertical 
for the Company. We intend to evaluate opportunities across 
this evolving ecosystem and leverage our existing technical 
capabilities, equipment, experience and industry relationships 
to participate meaningfully in this emerging sector. We strongly 
believe that this opportunity, together with our existing 
exploration businesses, has the potential to contribute to the 
next phase of  growth for South West Pinnacle.
Another area in which we take immense pride is safety. 
Exploration and drilling are inherently field-intensive activities, 
often undertaken in physically demanding and challenging 
environments. Safety, therefore, is not merely a policy or 
compliance requirement for us; it is an integral part of  our 
operating culture.
During the year, our teams delivered strong operational 
performance while maintaining an excellent safety record 
across our project sites. Every successful project begins with 
safe execution, and I would like to commend our employees, 
engineers, operators and site teams for making safety a shared 
responsibility across the organisation.
As we scale our operations, maintaining the highest standards 
of  health, safety and environmental responsibility will remain 
one of  our foremost priorities. We remain highly optimistic 
about the future of  your Company. Our healthy order book, 
strong execution track record, improving order mix, robust 
financial performance, established client relationships and 
committed workforce provide us with a strong foundation for 
the years ahead.
At the same time, the Government's increasing focus on 
domestic exploration, critical and strategic minerals, energy 
security and underground coal gasification is creating new 
opportunities across sectors in which South West Pinnacle has 
developed deep expertise.
We believe your Company is entering a phase of  accelerated 
and sustainable growth. Our focus will remain on disciplined 
execution, strengthening our core businesses, selectively 
expanding into high-potential areas, improving operational 
efficiencies and creating long-term value for all our 
stakeholders.
As we move forward, we will continue to pursue growth with 
the same principles that have guided us thus far  technical 
excellence, financial discipline, safety, integrity and an 
unwavering commitment to our clients.
I would like to express my sincere gratitude to our clients, 
employees, bankers, business partners, regulators and, most 
importantly, our shareholders, for your continued trust and 
support.
The progress of  South West Pinnacle is the result of  the 
collective commitment and efforts of  all our stakeholders. We 
assure you that we will continue to build this Company with 
discipline, integrity, ambition and a long-term vision, while 
striving to create sustainable value for all those associated with 
us.
I look forward to your continued support as we embark upon 
the next chapter of  South West Pinnacle's growth journey.
With Best Regards,
Piyush Jain 
Jt. Managing Director
Annual Report 2025-26  Ü IV

CSR INITIATIVES FY 2025-26At South West Pinnacle, we are committed to building a better tomorrow 
by empowering communities, promoting education, healthcare, and 
sustainable development through meaningful CSR initiatives.
EDUCATION SUPPORT
Supporting underprivileged children through 
educational materials, guidance and encouragement
to build a brighter future.
CLEAN WATER INITIATIVE
Providing safe and clean drinking water to 
communities through water coolers and 
purification systems.
WOMEN EMPOWERMENT
Promoting self-reliance and skill development 
through sewing machine distribution and 
vocational training.
COMMUNITY WELL-BEING
Contributing to the health and well-being 
of communities through essential facilities 
and support.
CHILD DEVELOPMENT
Engaging children through learning programs, 
activities and value education for holistic 
development.
COMMUNITY ENGAGEMENT
Promoting events and activities that 
strengthen community bonds and promote 
shared progress.
HEALTHCARE SUPPORT
Promoting basic healthcare and 
medical check-ups for a healthier 
community.
DISTRIBUTION DRIVES
Distribution drives for essential items 
to support those in need and bring smiles 
to many faces.
Building Values, Inspiring Change.

1. Pursuant to the applicable provisions of  the Companies Act, 
2013, the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2015 and the circulars issued by the Ministry of  
Corporate Affairs from time to time, the Annual General Meeting 
(AGM) of  the Company is being held through Video Conferencing 
(VC) / Other Audio-Visual Means (OAVM) without the physical 
presence of  Members at a common venue. The proceedings of  the 
AGM shall be deemed to be conducted at the Registered Office of  
the Company at Ground Floor, Plot No.15, Sector-44, Gurugram 
122003. The detailed procedure for participating in the meeting 
through VC / OAVM is appended herewith and also available at the 
Company's website www.southwestpinnacle.com.
2. Pursuant to the Circular No. 14/2020 dated April 08, 2020and 
amended thereof  issued by the Ministry of  Corporate Affairs, the 
facility to appoint proxy to attend and cast vote for the Members is 
not available for this AGM. However, the Body Corporates are 
entitled to appoint authorised representatives to attend the AGM 
through VC/OAVM and participate there at and cast their votes 
through e-voting.
3. The Register of  Directors and Key Managerial Personnel and 
their shareholding, maintained under Section 170 of  the Act and 
the Register of  Contracts or Arrangements in which the Directors 
are interested, maintained under Section 189 of  the Act, will be 
available electronically for inspection by the Members during the 
AGM. All the documents referred to in the Notice and 
Explanatory Statement, shall be available for inspection through 
electronic mode by the Members, in accordance with the applicable 
statutory requirements basis the request being sent on 
investors@southwestpinnacle.com.
4. The Company has engaged the services of  National Securities 
Depository Limited ('NSDL') as the authorised agency for 
conducting the AGM through VC / OAVM. The instructions for 
participation by Members are given in the subsequent paragraphs.
5. To prevent fraudulent transactions, Members are advised to 
exercise due diligence and notify the Company; any change in 
address or demise of  any Member as soon as possible. Members 
are also advised not to leave their demat account(s) dormant for 
long period. Periodic statement of  holdings should be obtained 
from the concerned Depository Participant ('DP') and holdings 
should be verified from time to time.
6. The relevant Explanatory Statement and reasons in respect of  
proposed Special Business (es) pursuant to Section 102 of  the 
Companies Act, 2013, are annexed hereto. The relevant details 
pursuant to Regulation 26(4) and 36(3) of  the Securities and 
Exchange Board of  India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure 
Requirements) Regulation 2015(“SEBI Listing Regulation”) and 
Secretarial Standard on General Meetings issued by the Institute of  
Company Secretaries of  India, in respect of  directors seeking 
appointment/reappointment at this AGM are also annexed 
herewith.
7. The Members can join the AGM in the VC/OAVM mode 15 
minutes before and after the scheduled time of  the 
commencement of  the Meeting by following the procedure 
mentioned in the Notice. The facility of  participation at the AGM 
through VC/OAVM will be made available for 1000 members on 
first come first served basis. This will not include large 
Shareholders (Shareholders holding 2% or more shareholding), 
Promoters, Institutional Investors, Directors, Key Managerial 
Personnel, the Chairpersons of  the Audit Committee, Nomination 
and Remuneration Committee and Stakeholders Relationship 
Committee, Auditors etc. who are allowed to attend the AGM 
without restriction on account of  first come first served basis.
8. The attendance of  the Members attending the AGM through 
VC/OAVM will be counted for the purpose of  reckoning the 
quorum under Section 103 of  the Companies Act, 2013.

Act, 2013 read with Rule 20 of  the Companies (Management and

19. The Members may further note that through SEBI 
Notification dated January 24, 2022, read with SEBI Circular dated 
January 25, 2022 and such other circular(s) issued and amended 
from time to time, the listed companies are required to issue the 
securities in dematerialised form only while processing the requests 
for Issue of  duplicate securities certificate, Claim from Unclaimed 
Suspense Account, Renewal / Exchange of  securities certificate, 
Endorsement, Sub-division/Splitting of  securities certificate, 
Consolidation of  securities certificates / folios, Transmission, 
Transposition. Also, in view of  the Regulation 40 of  the SEBI 
Listing Regulations, as amended from time to time, securities of  
listed companies can now be transferred only in the demat mode. 
Members holding shares in physical form are therefore requested 
to convert their holdings into the demat mode to avoid loss of  
shares or fraudulent transactions and avail better investor servicing.

directed to credit the dividend directly to the bank accounts of  the

automatically reflected in the Company's records.

(AGM) held on September 29, 2023, to hold office for a first term of  3 (three) consecutive years with effect from August 14, 2023, to August 
13, 2026, and whose office was not liable to retire by rotation.
Further, based on the recommendation of  Nomination and Remuneration Committee, the Board of  Directors in their meeting held on July 
20, 2026 has appointed Mrs. Meenakshi Anand (DIN: 01937203) as Independent Director, for a second term of  three years w.e.f. August 
14, 2026 to August 13, 2029. The Board suggested that based on the performance evaluation and recommendation of Nomination and 
remuneration committee, considers that given her background, experience and contribution, the continued association of  Mrs. Meenakshi 
Anand would be beneficial to the company and it is desirable to continue to avail her services as independent director. 
In the opinion of  the Board, Mrs. Meenakshi Anand fulfills the conditions specified in Sections 149, 152 and Schedule IV of  the Companies 
Act, 2013, Rules made thereunder and the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 and is independent 
on the management of  the Company. The Company has received the following declarations from Mrs. Meenakshi Anand:-

Directors) Rules, 2014 
(ii) Intimation in Form DIR-8 in terms of  the Companies (Appointment and Qualifications of  Directors) Rules, 2014, to the effect 
that she is not disqualified under Section 164(2) of  the Act
(iii)  Declaration to the effect that she meets the criteria of  independence as provided in Section 149(6) of  the Act read with 
Regulation 16 and Regulation 25(8) of  the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 as 
amended ('Listing Regulations') and 
(iv) Declaration that she has not been debarred from holding office of  a Director by virtue of  any Order passed by Securities and 
Exchange Board of  India or any other such authority.

DIVIDEND
THE COMPANY'S AFFAIRS 
The Board of  Directors of  the Company has decided to not to declare the dividend for the FY 2025-26, as it is considered expedient to 
redeploy its accruals into expanding the business. This approach aims to drive stronger growth in the coming years and ultimately deliver 
higher returns to its shareholders. 
SWPEL is an Integrated Service Provider, 
providing end-to-end drilling & exploration of  
natural resources viz. Coal, Ferrous, Non-
Ferrous & Atomic Minerals and Conventional & 
non- conventional Oil & Gas and ground water 
investigation. SWPEL's expertise also extends to 
carry out exploration using technological tools 
like surface geographical investigation, 
downhole geophysics, 2D/3D Seismic and 
Passive Seismic Tomography etc.
The coal block allocated to the Company in the 
state of  Jharkhand, which spread across 266 
Hectares, having Geological Reserves of  over 84 
million tons for which the Company has already 
signed a Coal Mine Development and 
Production Agreement with the Ministry of  
Coal, Government of  India.
Further, in exercise of  powers conferred under 
Mines and Mineral (Development and 
Regulation) Act 1957, Ministry of  Coal, 
Government of  India vide its notification dated 
26th November, 2025 published in Gazette of  
India on November 27, 2025, the Company has 
been notified as an accredited prospecting 
agencyby the Ministry of  Coal, Government of  
India, for carrying out prospecting operations 
for the exploration of  coal and Lignite. 
Accordingly, exploration activities at the Coal 
Block in Jharkhand have since been completed. 
GR Preparation and other activities are being 
undertaken on fast track mode.
Further, Company is having two JVs in Oman. 
The First one is imparting mining services pursuant to a long-term mining contract for Copper and Gold in Oman besides carrying out 
exploration and drilling services through a joint venture, namely, Alara Resources LLC., Oman. The second JV, namely, AL Hadeetha 
Mining LLC formed & has been allocated a mining block by Sultanate of  Oman wherein the exploration activities are in progress. 
The Company has successfully completed more than 165 projects during 19 years of  journey since inception for most of  the leading 
Government & Private Organizations and presently operating 20 projects on Pan India basis, in addition to JV Projects in Oman.
SWPEL has completed approximately 3.5 million meters of  drilling, 6.5 Lac meter of  Geo physical logging 515 sq.km. of  3 D Seismic 
surveys, 411 LKM of  2 D seismic Survey for exploration of  Coal, Mineral, Oil and Gas using 45state of  art drilling rigs having varied 
capacities to drill upto 2500 Meters depth and other equipment. 
Annual Report 2025-26  Ü 23
BOARD'S REPORT Exploration Limited

[page 40]
SWPE had received a Certificate of  Accreditation 
from National Accreditation Board for Education 
and Training (NABET), Quality Council of  India 
for preparation of  Comprehensive Geological 
Report (APA). It is a very prestigious accreditation 
giving the Company an edge over competitors in 
this domain. Its marquee clientele including 
Reliance industries, Vedanta, Oil India, Hindustan 
copper Ltd. Central Mine Planning and Design 
Institute Ltd., Central Ground Water Board and 
Hindalco, with operations spanning both 
domestic and select international markets.
Further, the Company has been empaneled by Oil 
India Limited for providing 2D/3D Seismic Data 
Acquisition Services across OIL's onshore 
acreages in India. The empanelment has been 
granted for a period of  three years and enables the 
Company to participate in bidding opportunities 
and tenders to be issued by Oil India Limited for 
seismic data acquisition services during the validity 
period of  the empanelment. This empanelment 
reflects the Company's technical expertise, 
operational capabilities, and experience in the field 
of  geophysical and seismic survey services. We 
believe that the empanelment will further 
strengthen our position in the exploration services 
sector and will enhance opportunities for 
participation in upcoming projects.
During the year under review, the Company has earned total income of  Rs. 24,651.61 lakhs as against Rs. 18,387.26 lakhs during the 
previous year with an increase of  approx. 35%. The year under review resulted in Net Profit after Tax of  Rs. 3,080 .05 lakhs as compared to 
Net Profit after Tax of  Rs. 1482.51 during the previous year registering a growth of  approx. 108% on standalone basis. 
On a consolidated basis, total income for FY 2025-26 stood at Rs. 24,662.46 lakhs as against Rs.18,514.28 lakhs during the previous year 
with an increase of  approx. 33% and Net Profit after Tax of  Rs. 3,302.70 Lakhs as compared to Net Profit after Tax of  Rs. 1,642.58  lakhs 
during the previous year registering a growth of   approx.100%.
In the preparation of  Financial Statements, no treatment from what is prescribed in the relevant Accounting Standards has been followed.
With the robust order book of  over approx. Rs. 761 crores and with participation in new tenders recently, the Company is confident of  
achieving the revenue growth ranging from 15% to 20% with corresponding increase in profits.  
The operations of  coal block of  the Company in the state of  Jharkhand by Ministry of  Coal and Mines, Government of  India, New Delhi, 
the which are falling under a separate segment i.e. coal business as per Ind-AS 108. Further as stated in the wake of  recent notification, the 
Company has been notified as an accredited prospecting agency by the Ministry of  Coal, Government of  India, for carrying out 
prospecting operations for the exploration of  coal and Lignite. Accordingly, exploration activities at the Coal Block in Jharkhand have since 
been completed. GR Preparation and other activities are being undertaken on fast track mode.

SIGNIFICANT FEATURES    
ISSUE OF EQUITY SHARES AND SHARES WARRANTS ON PREFRENTIAL BASIS
This joint venture was formed with Alara 
Resources Ltd., a renowned Australian 
exploration and mining company alongwith a 
local partner, in 2018 and the JV company was 
awarded an 11-year copper mining service 
contract in 2021–22, with a total contract 
value of  USD 125 million (approx. INR 1050 
Crores). Mining services operations 
commenced in February 2022 and are 
progressing well. The JV company is also 
executing various drilling and exploration 
contracts with 4 rigs, generating sizeable 
revenues and Profit.
SWPE had formed another joint venture namely AL Hadeetha Mining LLC (AHML), in Oman in the year 2024, comprising of  four 
partners, including Alara Resources Ltd., Australia.
In January 2025, AHML has been awarded an exploration cum mining block 22B, in IZKI Town, Oman for exploration and mining of  
minerals i.e. Copper, Gold, Silver & Chromite and Basalt.  As per agreement executed with Ministry of  Energy and Minerals, Sultanate of  
Oman, the JV Company has been granted the right to first explore and subsequently exploit the mining block, in accordance with terms set 
in the agreement. The exploration activities have since been started at rapid pace. Airborne geological survey has since been completed. 
Data analysis and report thereon is under preparation by the consultant. Since the mining block is very large in size, coupled with presence 
of  high value minerals positions it as promising opportunity for future development and economic value generations.

During the Financial Year 2024-25, the Company has made a preferential issue of  Equity Shares and Share Warrants at an issue price of  
₹132.20 per security (including a face value of  ₹10 per Equity Share), in accordance with the provisions of  the Securities and Exchange 
Board of  India (Issue of  Capital and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2018 ("SEBI ICDR Regulations"). The preferential issue was 
approved by the shareholders of  the Company at the Extraordinary General Meeting held on January 02, 2025.
Pursuant to the said approval, the Board of  Directors, at its meeting held on February 21, 2025, allotted 19,27,611 Equity Shares and 
28,21,411 Share Warrants (against receipt of  25% of  the issue price, as required under the SEBI ICDR Regulations) to persons belonging to 
the promoter and non-promoter categories.
Subsequently, upon receipt of  the balance consideration representing 75% of  the issue price per warrant, aggregating to ₹279,742,900 
(Rupees Twenty-Seven Crore Ninety-Seven Lakh Forty-Two Thousand and Nine Hundred Only), from the warrant holders pursuant to 
the exercise of  their option to convert the warrants into Equity Shares in accordance with the SEBI ICDR Regulations, the Board of  
Directors, at its meeting held on July 10, 2026, approved and allotted 28,21,411 Equity Shares upon conversion of  the outstanding Share 
Warrants to the respective promoter and non-promoter allottees.

value for its stakeholders.

The Company has established an internal control system 
commensurate with the size, scale, and complexity of  its operations. 
The Audit Committee comprises professionally qualified Directors 
who regularly interact with the Statutory Auditors, Internal Auditors, 
and management on matters within its terms of  reference. The 
Company has adequate systems of  internal control to ensure that 
transactions are duly authorised, accurately recorded and reported, and 
that its assets are safeguarded against loss, misuse, or unauthorised 
disposal.
The internal financial control framework encompasses the design, 
implementation, and maintenance of  adequate controls that operate 
effectively to ensure the orderly and efficient conduct of  business. 
These controls support adherence to the Company's policies, 
safeguarding of  assets, prevention and detection of  fraud and errors, 
accuracy and completeness of  accounting records, and timely 
preparation of  reliable financial information, in accordance with the 
applicable provisions of  the Act.
The Company has established documented policies, guidelines, and procedures to support the effective management of  internal financial 
controls while maintaining objectivity and independence. The Internal Auditor regularly monitors and evaluates the adequacy and 
effectiveness of  the internal control framework, including compliance with operating systems, accounting procedures, and policies across 
all locations of  the Company.

Sustainability has been embedded in SWPE's vision, mission, and business 
philosophy since its inception, reflecting the Company's commitment to 
responsible and sustainable growth. The Company has adopted a focused 
sustainability framework built around three key pillars: workplace sustainability, 
ecological stewardship, and ethical governance.
Through these pillars, SWPE focuses on fostering an inclusive and responsible 
workplace, promoting efficient use of  resources and environmental stewardship, 
and upholding high standards of  integrity, transparency, accountability, and 
compliance. The Company's commitment to responsible business development is 
further demonstrated through its alignment with relevant global and national sustainability frameworks. SWPE continues to integrate 
sustainability into its operations and decision-making to create long-term value for its stakeholders and contribute positively to society and 
the environment.

Report.
The Company's corporate governance framework is strengthened by its Code of  Conduct and Ethics, corporate governance guidelines, 
committee charters, and robust management processes. Its audit and internal control systems are aligned with the principles of  sound 
corporate governance, ensuring transparency, accountability, ethical conduct, and effective oversight. This Report provides a 
comprehensive overview of  the Company's governance practices and their alignment with the seven pillars of  its governance framework.

The Company has voluntarily provided the Integrated Report, which encompasses both financial and non-financial information to enable

Effective risk management is essential to success and is an integral part of  our culture. While there is a need to be accept a level of  risk in 
achieving our goals, the sound risk management helps the Company to make the most of  each business opportunity and enables it be 
resilient and respond decisively to the changing environment.
Approach to risk management assists the Company in identifying risks early and addressing them in ways that manage uncertainties, 
minimize potential hazards, and maximize opportunities for the good of  all our stakeholders including shareholders, customers, suppliers, 
regulators and employees. Risks can be broadly classified as Strategic, Operational, Financial, and Legal/Regulatory.
The Company is not required to maintain the cost record as specified by the Central Government under sub-section (1) of  section 148 of  
the Companies Act, 2013.
All pecuniary relationship or transactions of  the non-executive director's vis-a-vis the Company along with criteria for such payments and 
disclosures on the remuneration of  directors along with their shareholding are disclosed in this report.
Your Company has a Whistle Blower Policy and has established the necessary vigil mechanism in accordance with the act and SEBI (Listing 
Obligation and Disclosure Requirement) Regulations 2015. The Company's vigil mechanism / Whistle blower Policy aims to provide the 
appropriate platform and protection for Whistle-blowers to report instances of  any actual or suspected incidents of  unethical practices, 
violation of  applicable laws and regulations including the Integrity code, code of  conduct for Prevention of  Insider trading, code of  Fair 
Practices and Disclosure.
In compliance with the requirements of  Section 177 of  the Companies Act, 2013 and Regulation 22 of  SEBI (Listing Obligation and 
Disclosure Requirement) Regulation, 2015, the Company has established a vigil mechanism in form of  whistle Blower Policy for the 
Directors and Employees of  the Company through which genuine concerns regarding various issues can be communicated. 
The Company had adopted a Code of  conduct for Directors and Senior Management Executives (“the Code”), which lays down the 
principles and standards that should govern their actions.

ANNEXURE-III
MANAGEMENT DISCUSSION & ANALYSIS REPORT 
1. EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
2. GLOBAL ECONOMIC OVERVIEW
3. INDIAN ECONOMIC OVERVIEW
The fiscal year 2025-26 (FY26) represents a defining milestone in the corporate journey of  South West Pinnacle Exploration Limited 
(SWPEL). Amidst a powerful structural upswing in global resource exploration and intensified national mandates to enhance mineral 
security, the Company has scaled its operations to deliver the strongest operational and financial performance in its history.
During FY26, SWPEL achieved record consolidated operating revenues of  INR 2,430 Million, registering a robust 35% Year-on-Year 
growth over the previous fiscal. This growth was driven by high capacity utilization across our fleet of  45 state-of-the-art drilling rigs and 
expanding execution lines across our 20 active pan-India projects alongside our strategic Joint Ventures in the Sultanate of  Oman.
By maintaining a rigorous focus on project execution and strict cost management, our Consolidated Profit After Tax (PAT) doubled to INR 
330 Million, reflecting a significant expansion in our net profit margin to 13.58%. With an unprecedented, robust consolidated order book 
standing at INR 7613 Million as on date, SWPEL possesses exceptional multi-year revenue visibility.
We are successfully transitioning from a pure-play exploration services model to an asset-owning commercial developer, anchored by our 
commercial coal block in Jharkhand and our newly secured status as an accredited prospecting agency for coal and lignite exploration. 
SWPEL is exceptionally well-positioned to drive long-term value for all stakeholders.
The  current  Fiscal year  has not only  achieved major operational milestones, it expanded its technical boundaries too  .Our corporate 
strategy has positioned SWPEL exactly where macroeconomic necessity meets specialized execution capability. The global transition 
towards clean energy, the domestic infrastructure build out, and the intense push for critical mineral security require an unprecedented 
volume of  exploration, drilling and geophysical data. We have responded by scaling up our capabilities across CBM 
production/exploration, core mineral drilling, 2D/3D land seismic mapping, and underground drilling verticals.
Furthermore, the strategic breakthrough in being gazetted as an official accredited prospecting agency for coal and lignite exploration 
significantly fast-tracks the development of  our commercial coal asset in Jharkhand, opening up highly profitable long-term revenue 
streams expected to start in FY 2028-29.
The global economic landscape throughout 2025 and into the first half  of  2026 has been defined by structural shifts in manufacturing 
bases, supply chain realignments, and resource security. Despite lingering macro headwinds and variable inflationary environments across 
major economies, infrastructure spending and industrial production remain robust, driven by the global transition to clean energy and  
large-scale automation.
This reindustrialization era has initiated a powerful structural supercycle within the global metals, mining, and exploration services 
industries. Massive industrial demand for baseline non-ferrous commodities (such as copper, aluminum, and zinc) alongside critical battery 
minerals (including lithium, cobalt, gold, and rare earth elements) has forced corporate entities and sovereign governments to scale up 
exploratory capital expenditures. The race to map, quantify, and secure long-term primary reserves has shifted early-stage green field 
exploration from a cyclical operational choice to a top-tier national priority.
Consequently, the global exploration services market is expanding rapidly, on a clear trajectory to eclipse USD 18 billion by 2035 at a CAGR 
of  5.2% appox. Mineral-rich zones across the GCC region, Africa, Central Asia, and South America have emerged as high-priority, capital-
intensive hubs for multi-year deep drilling mandates, creating structural opportunities for technically qualified, mobile service providers.
India continues to stand out as one of  the fastest-growing major economies globally, demonstrating resilience and strong growth 
momentum into 2026. This economic growth is anchored by sustained public infrastructure investments, strong domestic consumption, 
and a clear policy focus on expanding the country's manufacturing base under central localization initiatives.
To support this rapid expansion, the country requires an uninterrupted supply of  primary energy and core industrial raw materials. Rapid 
urbanization, full-scale rural electrification, expanding transport networks, and the growth of  the electric vehicle (EV) and renewable 
energy ecosystems have placed heavy structural demands on India's primary resource extraction networks.
The Government of  India has recognized that reducing high import dependencies on energy and critical minerals is essential to sustaining 
long-term macroeconomic stability. This has triggered a complete transformation in the domestic resource sector, supported by major 
regulatory adjustments, fast-tracked clearances, and an active push to mobilize private corporate capital into early-stage geological 
exploration and commercial mining. The ongoing Iran –America conflict has reinforced the importance of  maximizing exploration and 
mining of  own resources  to make the nation  Atma Nirbhar.

4. INDUSTRY STRUCTURE & DEVELOPMENTS
5. GEOLOGICAL RESERVE POSITION (COAL & NON-COAL)
Coal Resources:
Strategic Non-Coal Minerals: 
6. MINING SECTOR CONTRIBUTION TO GDP
7. IMPORTANCE OF EXPLORATION IN INDIA
Key Strategic Objectives:
The domestic exploration and drilling services sector is undergoing a transformation, moving from legacy, low-depth methods to high-
precision, digital, and deep-cover exploration frameworks. The Indian exploration services market is currently valued between INR 15,000 
Crore and INR 18,000 Crore, expanding at a steady project-led CAGR of  8% to 10%. 
Historically, despite India possessing vast geographical reserves, drilling penetration depths remained low relative to international 
benchmarks, leaving deep-seated deposits unmapped. The current industry structure is shifting rapidly due to technical upgrades:
The integration of  advanced 2D/3D land seismic technologies, high-density wireline logging, 3D computerized geological visualization, 
and AI-driven predictive modeling has drastically reduced exploration turnaround times and optimized discovery hit-rates. The market is 
standardizing around fully integrated service operators that can provide end-to-end solutions—from initial surface geological mapping to 
deep core extraction and final volumetric reporting—enabling asset owners to compress project timelines.
India holds a strong geological reserve position globally, but these resources require extensive detailed drilling to transition from inferred 
assets into proven, commercially extractable reserves.
Ÿ
The country's estimated geological coal reserves stand at 344 BillionTonnes, reinforcing coal's role as the bedrock of  India's baseline energy 
mix. Domestic coal production crossed the historic 1 Billion Ton mark during FY 2024-25 and has maintained strong momentum into 
2026. However, total domestic demand continues to outpace localized supply, leading to a persistent supply gap that requires substantial 
coal imports, which stood at 268.24 MT.
Ÿ
For non-coal and critical minerals, India holds extensive unmapped deposits of  iron ore, bauxite, chromium, manganese ore, and rare earth 
elements. The mapping of  these deep-seated deposits represents a high-upside growth vertical for specialized core drilling operations.
The metals and mining sector serves as a core engine of  industrial GDP, directly feeding upstream manufacturing, heavy engineering, 
infrastructure development, and primary power production. Production indicators show that metallic mineral production reached USD 
14.69 Billion, while non-metallic mineral production stood at USD 1.57 Billion.
Geographically, the domestic mineral production and GDP contribution matrix remains highly concentrated across three key states:
Ÿ Odisha — Continues to lead as the premier mining state in the country, commanding a dominant 45% market share.
Ÿ Rajasthan — Holds the second position nationwide, contributing a firm 16% market share.
Ÿ Chhattisgarh — Remains a critical primary mining center, maintaining a 14% market share.
To optimize the mining sector's contribution to national GDP, the central policy focus has shifted toward fast-tracking early-stage 
prospecting. This strategy aims to bring hundreds of  newly discovered mineral blocks into active commercial production, expanding the 
order pipelines for integrated exploration companies.
Detailed geological exploration is a vital strategic necessity for India's economic resilience. Without high-density exploratory drilling and 
precise subsurface modeling, mining operations cannot be planned, capital cannot be efficiently deployed, and mineral recovery remains 
sub-optimal.
Ÿ Reducing Import Dependencies: India continues to import substantial volumes of  coal and essential base metals, leading to 
significant foreign exchange outflows. Accelerating domestic exploration directly targets resource self-reliance.
Ÿ Unlocking the Critical Mineral Pipeline: The domestic manufacturing of  electric vehicles, advanced energy storage systems, and 
semiconductor components requires a secure supply of  critical minerals and rare earth elements, which can only be unlocked via 
deep geophysical prospecting.
Ÿ De-risking Mining Capital Expenditures: High-precision 2D/3D land seismic surveys and core sampling de-risk large-scale 
investments for commercial miners by providing definitive data on deposit geometry, grade distribution, and strip ratios before 
mining begins.

8. GOVERNMENT POLICIES & REGULATORY FRAMEWORK
9. COMPANY OVERVIEW & BUSINESS MODEL
10. OPERATIONAL CAPABILITIES & SERVICE DOMAINS
A. CBM Exploration Services
B. CBM Production Drilling Services
C. Coal & Other Minerals Exploration Services
The Government of  India has introduced a series of  market-oriented regulatory reforms designed to remove administrative bottlenecks 
and encourage private sector participation in exploration.
Ÿ Mineral Block Auctions: Strategic allocation and fast-tracked commercial auctioning of  over 500 non-coal mineral blocks.
Ÿ FDI Allowances: Provision of  100% Foreign Direct Investment (FDI) under the automatic route for mineral mining, exploration, 
and upstream oil and gas sectors.
Ÿ Production Sharing Liberalization: Allowing captive miners to sell up to 50% of  their annual extraction in the open market, 
drastically upgrading project economics.
Ÿ National Survey Drivers: Broad deployment of  the Open Acreage Licensing Policy (OALP) alongside 'Mission Anveshan' to 
catalyze early-stage land seismic data acquisition.
Ÿ Launch of  Emergency Credit Line Guarantee Scheme 5 is another welcome step of  the Government to extend financial support 
to the companies like us , in the wake of  ongoing conflict in the Middle East Region. 
A crucial regulatory milestone directly benefiting SWPEL is the Central Government's focus on expanding the network of  approved 
exploration agencies. Following our official notification as an accredited prospecting agency for the exploration of  coal and lignite, our 
company is authorized to fast-track prospecting operations, create comprehensive Geological Reports (GR), and design formal mining 
plans. This accreditation enhances our operational authority and places us at the forefront of  the national exploration push.
Established in 2006, South West Pinnacle Exploration Limited (SWPEL) has grown into one of  India's premier private integrated 
exploration and mining services providers. Over the past 19 years, the Company has developed deep technical expertise in natural resource 
exploration, positioning it as a trusted partner for both large public sector undertakings (PSUs) and major private corporate conglomerates.
Our business model balances stable, service-fee-based cash flows from domestic drilling mandates with long-term revenue upside from 
asset ownership and international joint ventures.
SWPEL maintains an extensive array of  operational capabilities across eight  specialized engineering service domains:
Operational Scope: Identifying and evaluating Coal Bed Methane (CBM) potential in coal-rich basins.
Technical Process: Turnkey execution of  exploratory drilling, advanced wireline logging, core analysis, and comprehensive desorbed gas 
flow testing to establish definitive commercial asset viability.
Milestones: Completed over 60 core wells till date, establishing the single highest operational volume track record in India, with key 
relationships with Reliance Industries (RIL) etc.
Operational Scope: Designing, developing, operating, and maintaining specialized surface and subsurface production infrastructure to 
optimize continuous methane extraction.
Technical Process: Managed hydrogeological dewatering of  target coal seams to lower hydrostatic pressure, enabling the desorption and 
collection of  methane gas under real-time digital monitoring.
Milestones: Successfully drilled over 200 vertical wells and 60 directional drilling wells having 187 laterals upto a depth of  2406 meters.. 
SWPEL operates as an exclusive partner to RIL, utilizing our specialized fleet of  advanced CBM drilling rigs to scale up production.
Operational Scope: Turnkey Greenfield and brown field prospecting for coal, ferrous, non-ferrous, and atomic minerals.
Technical Process: Multidisciplinary work flows including topography mapping, core sampling, Reverse Circulation (RC), Down-The-
Hole (DTH), mud rotary drilling, managed blasting, and large-scale Overburden (OB) removal.
Milestones: Completed approximately 3.5 million (35 Lakh) cumulative meters of  drilling, supported by our versatile fleet of  45 deep-
capacity rigs.

I. UNDERGROUND COAL GASIFICATION
11. ORDER BOOK POSITION & REVENUE VISIBILITY
Operational Scope: High-fidelity geophysical structural mapping to identify oil, gas, coal and other deep-seated mineral formations.
Technical Process: Propagating controlled acoustic waves into the ground and capturing subsurface reflections via dense sensor arrays to 
build detailed 2D profiles and 3D digital block models.
Milestones: Proven land seismic execution tracking 515 sq. km. of  3D Seismic surveys and 411 LKM of  2D seismic surveys, fully 
equipped with advanced PST Technology for data processing and analysis.
Operational Scope : Passive seismic tomography is a geophysical method that constructs 3D subsurface images by measuring natural or 
ambient vibrations (like micro-earthquakes, ocean waves, or cultural noise) instead of  using artificial sources. 
Technical Process: It maps geological structures by analyzing the speed and paths of  seismic waves as they travel through the earth. 
Instead of  using heavy equipment like dynamite or vibroseis trucks to create vibrations, this technique uses a portable network of  
seismometers or nodal geophones to "listen" to the ambient noise of  the Earth.
Milestones: Successfully completed two large projects of  Oil India over a period of  last three years.
Operational Scope: Comprehensive studies of  Earth's structure to guide corporate mining layouts and infrastructure development.
Technical Process: Surface geological mapping paired with subsurface electrical resistivity and seismic surveys to interpret lithological 
formations.
Milestones: Driven by a core team of  43 experienced geoscientists, completing over 6.5 Lakh meters of  geophysical logging across 
projects.
Operational Scope: Mapping underground water-bearing formations to evaluate hydrogeological quantity and quality.
Technical Process: Combining remote sensing, deep geophysical soundings, and exploratory pump testing to deliver detailed 
groundwater models.
Milestones: Successfully completed regional aquifer management mapping programs for state namely Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra and 
Gujarat. and national water resource departments, including the Central Ground Water Board (CGWB).
Operational Scope: Creating high-precision boreholes from within subterranean mines and confined spaces for exploration or utility 
routing.
Technical Process: Deploying specialized, compact underground drilling rigs into confined workspaces to extract intact core samples 
from deep rock strata.
Milestones: Expanded our underground drilling division by deploying  advanced, specialized rigs under a commercial contract from 
Hindustan Copper Ltd.
Operational Scope: It includes geological mapping, coal seam correlation, thickness, moisture etc before drilling vertical, directional or 
horizontal wells 
Technical Process: For underground coal gasification one needs to combine deep coal core drilling, geological and hydrogeological 
characterisation, downhole logging, resource modelling and specialised drilling support 
SWPEL entered the 2026-27 fiscal year with its strongest order book to date, reflecting a successful bidding strategy and high market 
demand. As on date, the Company's robust consolidated order book crossed INR Million 7613 (INR 761 Crores).
This order book is distributed across our active pan-India projects, ensuring highly predictable asset utilization and a strong revenue 
pipeline in near to medium term.

14. CLIENT SATISFACTION & RETENTION
A key element of  SWPEL's long-term growth strategy is our transition into asset ownership through our commercial coal block in the State 
of  Jharkhand.
Ÿ Reserve Parameters: The assigned mining block covers 2.66 sq. km. and contains estimated geological reserves of  84 
MillionTonnes (MT) of  high-demand W-IV grade coal.
Ÿ Accreditation and Exploration Phase: Following the Gazette notification as an accredited prospecting agency for coal and 
lignite by Government of  India, SWPEL has initiated physical on-site exploration and mine development operations.
Ÿ Regulatory Milestones: The digital Differential Global Positioning System (DGPS) survey is fully complete.
Ÿ Present Status :The Exploration activities have been completed and preparation of  definitive Geological report (GR) is 
underway and is expected to be submitted soon.
Ÿ Next Steps & Production Timeline: Upon submission of  GR, the formal mining plan will be fast-tracked alongside 
environmental clearances.
Ÿ We are targeting the commencement of  commercial coal production by FY 2028–29. 
Ÿ This asset is projected to generate scalable revenues of  INR 700 to INR 800 Crores over the first three years of  production, 
operating with estimated EBITDA margins of  40% to 42%.
SWPEL has built a strong international presence in the Sultanate of  Oman through two strategic corporate joint ventures, positioning the 
company to benefit from the country's mineral diversification initiatives.
Our first international joint venture entity was awarded a major 11-year copper mining contract with a total contract value of  USD 125 
million. Commercial mining services operations officially commenced in February 2022 and continue to progress smoothly, generating 
steady, scalable revenues.
Besides the JV is also imparting exploration services to the local clients using its 4 owned rigs there.
Overall our mining and exploration service operations in Oman continue to  perform well, and we project a stable annual net margin 
between 5% and 6% from this geography. 
Expanding our international footprint, a second joint venture was formally established, comprising four strategic partners, including the 
highly reputed Alara Resources Ltd., Australia. Under the definitive concession agreement executed directly with the Ministry of  Energy 
and Minerals, Sultanate of  Oman,  this JV has been granted exclusive rights to first explore and subsequently exploit a massive mining block 
comprising of  minerals ,mainly  copperbesides  manganese , basalt , zinc etc. 
The global copper market is expected to remain fundamentally strong. Robust demand from electrification, renewable energy, electric 
vehicles, power infrastructure and AI-driven digital infrastructure is likely to outpace supply growth constrained by declining ore grades, 
lengthy mine development timelines and geopolitical risks. This structural imbalance is expected to support sustained investment in copper 
exploration, mining and processing, making copper one of  the most strategically important commodities of  the coming decade.
The operationalization of  the recently awarded mineral block through our second JV thus provides an exceptionally positive long-term 
outlook for our international revenues.
SWPEL's corporate growth is built on strong institutional client retention across both public sector undertakings (PSUs) and premier 
private sector conglomerates. Our ability to deliver complex projects under challenging geological conditions has established SWPEL as a 
preferred partner for critical mandates.
Ÿ Public Sector Retention: Multi-year contracts and repeat orders from top-tier institutional clients including CMPDI, Hindustan 
Copper Ltd., Oil India Limited (OIL), and  Central Ground Water Board (CGWB).

Ÿ Private Sector Alignment: Preferred-vendor and production partner framework with Reliance Industries Limited (RIL) for

Ÿ Our focus on engineering accuracy and timely contract completion has helped us maintain a high retention rate, de-risking our

Operating high capacity drilling equipment and managing exploration works at remote and difficult terrains require a strong corporate 
commitment to health, safety, and environmental protocols. SWPEL maintains an exemplary safety benchmark with a Zero Lost Time 
Injury (LTI) record across its history.
Ÿ Rigorous Field Training: Continuous safety induction programs and real-time hazard identification protocols for all field 
engineers and drilling crews.
Ÿ Standardized Equipment Maintenance: Comprehensive preventative maintenance schedules across our fleet of  45 drilling 
rigs to ensure mechanical safety and integrity.
Ÿ Environmental Stewardship: Transitioning toward low-impact, scientific exploration techniques that minimize the 
environmental footprint on site, ensuring alignment with tightening ESG and statutory requirements.
The fiscal year 2025-26 represents the strongest financial performance in the company's history, demonstrating significant growth and 
improved operating leverage.
Ÿ Operating Income: Consolidated revenue from operations reached INR 2,430 Million, representing a strong 35% Year-on-
Year growth compared to the INR 1,803 Million recorded in FY 2024-25.
Ÿ Consolidated EBITDA: Surged by 73.5% to INR 583 Million (up from INR 336 Million in FY25), expanding our full-year 
EBITDA Margin to 23.99% due to strong asset utilization and efficient cost management.
Ÿ Net Profit After Tax (PAT): Grew by 101% YoY to INR 330 Million (up from INR 164 Million in FY25), yielding an improved 
consolidated PAT Margin of  13.58%.
This strong profitability was achieved through proactive corporate expense management combined with improved operating leverage 
across our expanded revenue base.

19. RISK MANAGEMENT FRAMEWORK
Ÿ Full-Spectrum Services Portfolio: Integrated capabilities across high-barrier drilling, seismic mapping, CBM extraction, and 
specialized underground exploration verticals.
Ÿ Proven Operational Track Record: Successful completion of  165+ complex projects with over 3.5 million meters drilled across 
diverse terrains.
Ÿ Versatile Equipment Base: Complete ownership of  a modern fleet of  45 specialized drilling rigs.
Ÿ Strong Balance Sheet: Strong financial positioning with a conservative 0.39 Debt/Equity ratio and an expanding 23% ROCE.
Ÿ Strategic Resource Assets: Direct ownership of  our commercial Jharkhand coal block and 
Ÿ Growing presence in Oman via two active JVs.
Ÿ Competitive Bidding Framework: Operating within a tender-based procurement system can create pricing pressures during 
competitive bidding rounds.
Ÿ Dependency on Statutory Clearances: Project execution timelines can be affected by standard timelines for obtaining forest, 
environmental, and regional clearances.
Ÿ Weather Sensitivity: Field drilling velocity and seasonal seismic surveys remain subject to weather conditions and monsoon 
disruptions.
Ÿ Expanding Mineral Block Auctions: The Central Government's mandate to auction 500+ non-coal mineral blocks expands 
our project pipeline.
Ÿ Upstream Energy Initiatives: 100% FDI allowances in the oil & gas sector paired with active OALP and Mission Anveshan 
rollouts drive domestic seismic exploration.
Ÿ National Aquifer Mandates: The shift toward scientific groundwater management via the CGWB creates opportunities for 
large-scale hydrogeological mapping contracts.
Ÿ GCC Region Expansion: Significant mineral potential within our dual joint ventures in Oman.
Ÿ  Copper is increasingly viewed as the "new oil of  electrification." Governments and mining companies are accelerating 
exploration and mine development, but the long lead times mean new supply will take years to materialize. For exploration and 
drilling companies, this creates significant opportunities because:
Ÿ  More greenfield and brownfield exploration will be required. 
Ÿ  Existing mines need resource expansion through continued drilling. 
Ÿ Evolving Environmental Regulations: Changes in environmental compliance standards or sudden adjustments in legislative 
frameworks.
Ÿ Macro-Policy and Fiscal Volatility: Unanticipated adjustments to baseline tax regulations, royalty matrix structures, or 
operational mining guidelines.
Ÿ Localized Field Obstacles: Occasional localized logistical delays or non-cooperation from community clusters, which can 
temporarily impact site mobilization.
SWPEL utilizes an enterprise-wide risk management framework to identify, assess, and mitigate operational and financial risks before they 
impact performance.
Ÿ Tender Pricing Pressures — Addressed by shifting asset weight toward specialized, high-barrier technical segments where 
competition is scarce.
Annual Report 2025-26  Ü 47
MANAGEMENT DISCUSSION AND ANALYSIS REPORT
Exploration Limited
[page 64]
Ÿ Statutory Clearance Pipeline — Controlled by specialized internal legal and land groups executing compliance files ahead of  
physical equipment schedules.
Ÿ Macro-Currency Exposure — Handled by framing secure, long-term international joint venture metrics around stable, dollar-
denominated copper extraction assets in Oman.
The Company operates an internal control framework that is fully commensurate with the size, scope, and operational complexity of  its 
business. We utilize system-enforced workflows accompanied by a strict, multi-tiered corporate authorization matrix to ensure all financial 
and operational transactions are executed under proper management review.
SWPEL employs a dedicated, qualified Internal Auditor alongside specialized field compliance personnel who closely monitor day-to-day 
operations to ensure adherence to our standard operating procedures (SOPs). The Internal Auditor submits independent review reports 
periodically, which are reviewed by executive management, the Audit Committee, and the Board. Furthermore, our internal audit team 
collaborates closely with our Statutory Auditors to ensure accurate transaction recording, rigorous physical inventory counts, and 
verification of  the company's fixed assets and rig machinery.
As a specialized, technical service provider, SWPEL considers its workforce to be a core competitive asset. The Company is supported by a 
highly skilled technical workforce, with our current head-count standing at  dedicated corporate and field employees. We continue to 
maintain a low employee attrition rate that sits well below the broader engineering and mining industry averages.
The company's track record for timely project delivery is driven by our field engineers and crew members, who execute complex drilling 
schedules within tight timelines under diverse field conditions. This commitment has earned the company strong client testimonials from 
major PSU and corporate asset owners. Management remains committed to nurturing an open, transparent corporate culture where 
continuous dialogue is encouraged, and employee insights are integrated into operational planning. Relations across all operating project 
sites remain positive and cooperative.
The intermediate and long-term outlook for SWPEL remains positive across all operating divisions. Backed by a healthy consolidated order 
book of  INR 7613 Million, our core exploration service business lines are projected to sustain a steady compound growth rate of  15% to 
20% per annum over the near to medium term, with core EBITDA margins expected to remain in the range of  24-25 %. Long-term growth 
will be supported by the commercialization of  our Jharkhand coal block and the expansion of  our mining concessions in the Sultanate of  
Oman .
Ÿ To strengthen operational capacity and technology adoption 
Ÿ To deepen our presence in emerging exploration segments 
Ÿ  To maintain industry-leading safety standards 
Ÿ  To improve margins through better execution efficiency
Ÿ  To remain focussed on Oman Business 
Ÿ  To operationalise Coal block as early as possible 
Ÿ  And above all, to deliver sustainable value to our shareholders.
Keeping above in mind, we remain focused on expanding our capabilities with experienced manpower, maintaining execution excellence 
on pan India basis, and creating sustainable value for all stakeholders.

ANNEXURE-IV
REPORT ON CORPORATE GOVERNANCE
COMPANY'S PHILOSOPHY ON CORPORATE GOVERNANCE
The Company's corporate governance framework is reinforced through its Code of  Conduct and Ethics, Corporate Governance 
Guidelines and the Charters of  the Committees constituted by the Board of  Directors of  the Company (“Board”). The Board and 
management processes, audits and internal control systems reflect the principles of  the Company's corporate governance framework.
This Corporate Governance Report of  South West Pinnacle Exploration Limited (“SWPE” or “the Company”) for the financial year 
ended March 31, 2026, has been prepared in accordance with the applicable provisions of  the Securities and Exchange Board of  India 
(Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (“SEBI Listing Regulations”), as amended from time to time, read 
with the applicable provisions of  the Companies Act, 2013 (“the Act”) and the rules made thereunder, as amended from time to time. This 
Report forms an integral part of  the Integrated Annual Report of  the Company.
SWPE believes in transparency, empowerment, accountability and integrity in its operations. The Company follows a framework of  
delegated authority under which various functional heads are entrusted with appropriate responsibilities for achieving the Company's 
business objectives, with the ultimate aim of  enhancing stakeholder value.
The Company believes that sound corporate governance practices provide an important foundation for the Board and management to 
discharge their responsibilities effectively. The Board is responsible for safeguarding the long-term interests of  all stakeholders while 
adhering to sound principles of  corporate governance. The Company has professionals on its Board who actively participate in 
deliberations on significant policy and strategic matters.
The Company's corporate governance framework reflects its values, culture, policies and relationships with its stakeholders. Integrity and 
transparency are integral to the Company's governance practices and are fundamental to maintaining the trust and confidence of  its 
stakeholders.
The Company regards corporate governance as an ethically driven business process committed to values, responsible conduct and 
sustainable value creation. This is achieved through ethical decision-making and conducting business with a strong commitment to integrity 
while seeking to meet the expectations of  all stakeholders.
Good governance practices emanate from the culture and mindset of an organisation. SWPE is committed to meeting the aspirations 
of  all its stakeholders and adopting sound governance practices for the ethical and responsible conduct of its business. The Company 
recognises that an effective Board is a prerequisite for strong and effective corporate governance. The Board and its Committees are 
constituted in accordance with the applicable provisions of  the Companies Act, 2013 and the SEBI Listing Regulations and oversee 
the manner in which the management serves and protects the long-term interests of  all stakeholders.

Our Core Values
ROLE OF THE BOARD OF DIRECTORS
COMPOSITION OF BOARD OF DIRECTORS
Our core values are summarised below:
Ÿ Commitment to excellence and customer satisfaction;
Ÿ Maximising long-term shareholder value;
Ÿ Being a socially responsible enterprise; and
Ÿ Caring for people and the environment.
Ÿ Strong leadership and effective corporate governance practices 
The Company has adopted a Code of  Conduct applicable to its employees and members of  the Board, including the Managing Director, 
Executive Directors and Non-Executive Directors. The Company has also adopted a Code of  Conduct for Independent Directors, 
incorporating the duties and responsibilities of  Independent Directors as prescribed under the Companies Act, 2013.Further, the 
Company complies with the applicable corporate governance requirements stipulated under Regulations 17 to 27 read with Schedule V and 
Regulation 46(2), as applicable, of  the SEBI Listing Regulations. The details relating to the composition of  the Board and the various 
Committees constituting the governance structure of  the Company are set out in detail in this Report.
The primary role of  the Board is that of  trusteeship. As trustees, the Board has a fiduciary responsibility to ensure that the Company 
has clear goals aligned to the members value and its growth. Further, the Board is also responsible for:
Ÿ Exercising appropriate control to ensure that the Company is managed efficiently to fulfil stakeholders' aspirations and 
societal expectations.
Ÿ  Monitoring the effectiveness of  the Company's governance practices and making changes as necessary.
Ÿ  Providing strategic guidance to the Company and ensuring effective monitoring of  the Management. 
Ÿ  Exercising independent judgment on corporate affairs. 
Ÿ  Assigning enough numbers of  non-executive members of  the Board to tasks where there is a potential for conflict of  interest in 
the exercise of  independent judgment. 
The Company recognizes and embraces the importance of  a diverse Board to leverage differences in thought, perspective, regional and 
industry experience, cultural and geographical background, age, ethnicity, race, gender, knowledge, skills including expertise in financial, 
global business, leadership, technology, mergers and acquisition, strategy, sales and marketing, Environmental, Social and Governance 
(ESG), risk and cybersecurity and other domains, which will ensure that SWPE retains its competitive advantage. 
The Board exercises strategic oversight over business operations while directly measuring and rewarding management's performance. 
Simultaneously, the Board ensures compliance with the legal framework, integrity of  financial accounting and reporting systems and 
credibility in the eyes of  the stakeholders through proper and timely disclosures.
The composition of  the Board Directors is in conformity with the provisions of  the Companies Act, 2013 read with Regulation 17 of  the 
Securities and Exchange Board of  India (Listing Obligation and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 as amended from time to 
time.
(i) Presently, the Board consists of  Six Directors with an optimum combination of  Executive, Non-Executive and Independent 
Directors. The Board meets regularly and is responsible for the proper direction and management of  the Company. The 
Company takes the proper disclosures from all the Directors including Independent Directors as laid down in Act.
(ii) None of  the Directors on the Board holds directorships in more than ten public companies. Also, none of  them is a member 
of  more than ten committees or chairman of  more than five committees across all the public companies in which he/she is a 
director. Necessary disclosures regarding Committee positions in other public companies as on March 31, 2026 have been 
made by the Directors. 
None of  the Directors is related to other directors, except Mr. Vikas Jain, Chairman & Managing Director, who is elder brother of  
Mr. Piyush Jain, Joint Managing Director of  Company.

Objectives of  the Committee 
Meeting / Attendance
Terms of  Reference
Extract of  Terms of  Reference: -
The Audit Committee of  the Company is consisted of  two Non-Executive Independent Directors and one whole time Director. The 
chairman of  the committee is an independent Director having financial and accounting Knowledge.
The primary objective of  the Committee is to assist the Board with oversight of:
1. The accuracy, integrity and transparency of  the Company's financial statements with adequate and timely disclosures.
2. Compliance with legal and regulatory requirements.
3. The Company's independent auditors' professional qualifications and independence.
4. The performance of  the Company's independent auditors and internal auditors; and 
5. Acquisitions and investments made by the Company.
The Composition of  Audit Committee is as follows:-
The Company Secretary acts as the secretary to the Audit Committee.
During the financial year ended 31 March, 2026, the Audit Committee of  the Company met four times. The dates of  the meetings were May 
22, 2025, August 12, 2025, October 29, 2025, and February 09, 2026;
i)    The requisite quorum was present at all meetings.
ii)   The Company Secretary functions as the Secretary of  the Committee.
iii) The Minutes of  the Meetings of  the audit committee are discussed and taken note of  by the Board of  Directors.
iv) The meetings of  the Committee are also attended by the Statutory Auditors, Internal Auditor, CFO and Company Secretary.
th
iv) The Chairman of  the Audit Committee was present at the 19  Annual General Meeting of  the Company held on September 
26, 2025 to answer member queries.
In accordance with Section 177(1) of  the Companies Act, 2013 and as per the requirements of  SEBI (Listing Obligation & Disclosure 
requirements) Regulations 2015, the Board of  director have approved terms of  reference for the Audit Committee in conformity with the 
required Regulation 18 of  SEBI (Listing Obligation & Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 and Section 177(1) of  the Companies 
Act, 2013. Further the Audit Committee has been granted powers as prescribed under Regulation 18(2) of  SEBI (Listing Obligation & 
Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 as amended.
a) Review of  financial reporting process.
b) Reviewing with the management, the annual financial statements and auditors' report thereon before submission to the Board 
for approval.
c) Evaluation of  internal financial controls and risk management systems.
d) Recommendation for appointment, remuneration and terms of  appointment of  statutory and internal auditors of  the 
Company.
e) Approve policies in relation to the implementation of  the insider trading code and to supervise implementation of  the same.
f)  Review and monitor the auditor's independence, performance and effectiveness of  audit process.

8. Leadership development

10. Devise a policy on Board diversity.

DIRECTORS REMUNERATION AND POLICY:
